Its reproduced Markdown exact-add gap was repaired by DEC-78's project-path/exclusion boundaries,then DEC-79's scoped literal declaring directories;DEC-85 now completes the remaining compatible legacy-root/shared absolute-selector reconciliation. Discovery-only Markdown suppression now preserves explicit CLI/RPC paths and stronger legacy Core denials (DEC-81;2,995 units/37 parity/production). Definitive credential authority now repairs that reproduced bootstrap defect and completes all eight startup-option clauses: CLI-RUN-07 closes under DEC-82 (3,032 units/48 parity/production), without closing broader TUI/settings/provider or resource-API criteria. Optional built-in controls remain delivered (DEC-71); their earlier whole-criterion closure is superseded, not their implementation or retained evidence. Preceding native failures remain unexplained. Typed extensions and bounded directory manifests reach 2,443 units/32 parity without further criterion closure (DEC-72/73). The earlier eight-contract Web delivery (DEC-67) retains its complete nine-suite browser evidence; historical CLI/Core checkpoints do not independently certify later browser compositions. No complete Pi-parity/release acceptance is claimed. RPC extension UI has bounded plain-text presentation, immutable capabilities, ordered replay, same-ID update/close and lifecycle cancellation (CLI-AUT-07); broader TUI extension capabilities remain open. Canonical default-workspace validation, display-only home labels, bounded revalidated selection persistence and explicit unavailable-default recovery are now delivered (WEB-FILE-02). Capture-phase stacked Escape and coarse-safe focus are now delivered (WEB-CHAT-10). In addition to the prior CLI/Web/MCP deliveries, Core now owns the persistent per-session MCP host and a shared deferred-tool loadout used by CLI, RPC and Web. `tool_search` ranks bounded metadata without declaring candidate schemas, waits for indirect MCP metadata only on demand, refreshes definitions for the next provider turn, and persists host-authenticated activations on the active session branch. Current preset, allowlist, exclusion, extension and MCP read-only policy is reapplied after resume, fork, navigation and extension reload; a same-response undeclared call remains rejected. Core now also owns encrypted MCP OAuth state, detached loopback/paste flows and passive CLI/RPC/Web refresh. Its behavioral/security tests and reviewed desktop/mobile screenshots pass, and WEB-MCP-10 now closes with bilingual user/operator guidance and eight versioned OAuth visual baselines. Core now also owns the separate bounded QuickJS guest sandbox, live authorized nested calls and transactional branch store; CLI MCP login/logout, hidden/per-tool exposure and scoped Settings reasons have behavioral and reviewed visual evidence. The classifier/image bridges, specialized nested progress/cost cards, MCP command ownership/streaming routing and dynamic child-control retention are now delivered through bounded AiHarness-owned contracts. Interrupted-tool accounting now persists before terminal stop, reasoning resolves at each new model request, and stable active-run copies plus send-time history edits have behavioral and reviewed responsive evidence. Independent new-session defaults, extension startup options, shared Markdown semantics, the high-level session SDK, actual journal-backed terminal status, source-authorized literal npm commands, exact scoped provider-group model batches, independent skill-group frontmatter batches with owned rollback and an offline synthetic static demo are now delivered. Linux-scoped outside-root linked-directory consent and its responsive optional controls are now delivered without extending trust, roots or execution authority. Native detached Web progress/replay and reply evidence now qualify independently; crash-durable running progress (CLI-TOOL-10), broader TUI/automation, recorded history regressions and remaining Pi Web capabilities stay open. The guarded Pages pipeline exists but no remote deployment is proved.

The [current completion review](./acceptance/completion-review.md) maps the user objective to retained artifacts and enumerates every remaining criterion. Its result is explicitly **not complete**; scoped green tests never substitute for missing requirements.

### Scope rules

- **CLI parity is a separate track.** It covers invocation, interactive behavior, commands, providers, configuration, sessions, tools, resources, extensions, automation, SDK integration, and security documented by Pi `1.0.0`.
- **Web scope is delta-only.** It does not recount all Pi Web `0.9.3` behavior. Existing AiHarness equivalents are recorded only as foundations for a behavior added or materially changed in `0.10.0`.
- Similar names do not prove functional parity, and different names do not disprove it. Provider, extension, JSONL, RPC, session, and SDK behavior is assessed through documented AiHarness-owned interfaces.
- Upstream public compatibility is out of scope. No criterion requires upstream names, directories, environment variables, command spelling, wire envelopes, persisted formats, source-level extension loading, package imports, or drop-in SDK substitution.
- Each criterion groups one independently closable capability. Annex B maps all changed upstream file families into the Web criteria; individual upstream tests are behavioral evidence, not separate criteria.

### Known limitations

- Continuation checkpoint (2026-10-13): local commits `62c5d52` (MCP lock handoff) and `8524422` (RPC UI) now retain versioned [MCP acceptance](./acceptance/mcp-lock-handoff.md) and [RPC acceptance](./acceptance/rpc-extension-ui.md). Default `make test` passes214 files/2,132 tests; types, strict added-test types, zero-warning owning lint and80 focused cases pass. Standalone parity initially fails fresh CLI compilation with SIGSEGV/139; its separately recorded whole-target reproduction passes32 unchanged cases. CPU-affinity diagnosis remains failed (unchanged child Zod import reports a non-constructor superclass); no native root cause or repair is claimed. The complete browser checkpoint executes533 cases:531 pass, one strict tablet-light history anchor assertion fails and one genuine progress worker exits SIGSEGV. Logs stay under `/tmp/aih-ready-*-4.*`; complete reports are retained in `/tmp/aih-cont-prior-browsers.tar.gz`. These are scoped local commits, not remote publication or complete parity/release acceptance.
- History follow-up retains two instrumented controlled diagnostics (17/18 and16/18 pass). They establish that the16px discrepancy already exists at pointerdown and in the pagination handler's captured offset: a second Playwright locator reveal moves the padded root between the earlier measurement and click. Restoration correctly preserves that later captured offset. The final test reveals once, then issues a real pointer click without a second locator reveal; all18 six-viewport repeated cases pass with the same3px assertion and unchanged captures/tolerances. This fixes test input sequencing, not a new product restoration algorithm. The subsequent complete final533-case matrix independently passes all six strengthened history cases; repeated focused success alone is not used as complete browser acceptance.
- Source review finds a separate genuine failure-evidence defect: appending a failed visible reply could launch automatic compaction without the run signal. A real JSONL regression fails on the preceding candidate (one failure/eight passes) because the summary callback runs, and passes after failure-only `skipAutoCompaction: true` (nine passes). The error reply remains durable, unbilled and unsuccessful; retained context stays unchanged. `/tmp/aih-cont-failure-{red,green}.*` records both results. This last Core correction postdates the214/2,132 gate. Fresh image `sha256:a414321d146630fbaf335f9b04198eaf0ff5342d524e3a7aeee9ba6029ac380f` matches all38 current owners and passes workspace/parity types, strict12-leaf test types, zero-warning37-owner lint, complete214 files/2,133 tests and standalone parity32. Its complete retained-container browser matrix passes all533 cases (354 standard,69 interactions,8 locale,11 workspace,14 pane,43 linked,2 title,24 progress,8 demo), actual exit0/OOMfalse, one worker/zero retries/no capture updates. All38 actual browser-container source hashes match the current tree (`/tmp/aih-cont-integrated-browser.*`, artifacts under `/tmp/aih-cont-integrated-browser-artifacts/`). This qualifies the current lot, not the64 remaining requirements or a native-crash repair. The preceding Make browser composition separately passes all354 standard/eight demo cases, but interactions exits without a test summary and six siblings refuse leftover occupied ports. That nonzero checkpoint is retained, not diagnosed or counted as complete acceptance.

- Current maintenance preserves all preceding evidence. Web unread presentation now follows the last observed tail ID rather than loaded-window growth; five units cover older/overlapping pages, genuine appended entries and missing identity. The unchanged-product long-history browser control also passes the stronger unread assertion, so that journey is conservation evidence, not a causal counter control.
- `/tmp/aih-ready-full-1.*` remains nonzero:2,132 assertions pass but only213 of214 files complete, with installed-executable worker SIGABRT and native `FSReqAfterScope` assertion `(wrap_->req()) == (req)`. `/tmp/aih-ready-full-2.*` remains nonzero:213 files/2,126 pass, six installed cases skipped after genuine fresh Core `tsc` SIGSEGV/139. ## 3. Traceability by Surface

For every row, completion requires all named behavior, automated behavioral coverage, migration coverage where persisted AiHarness state changes, and applicable user documentation and visual evidence. The final parenthetical codes point to Annex A. A foundation without the complete capability is `PARTIAL`; differences limited to upstream public naming or serialization do not keep a capability open.

### 3.1 Pi 1.0.0 CLI parity

#### Invocation and built-in commands

| ID | Status | Criterion and current evidence |
|---|---|---|
| CLI-RUN-01 | `DONE` | Provide the installed executable contract, strict option parsing, extension-contributed help, `--help`, and `--version`. AiHarness now has strict built-in option parsing, metadata-only help/version exits, fail-fast unknown options, and golden process tests; trust-gated extension options/help/completion and fresh generations are delivered. Genuine local npm archives now install outside a read-only checkout with lifecycle scripts disabled and network unavailable; PATH resolves only the installed bin, pack/dist bytes and worker/WASM assets match real builds, and installed print/JSON/RPC use unchanged goldens. Public Node/NodeNext consumers exercise fresh CommonJS reload, useful SDK and QuickJS. Private manifests prohibit public registry publication, not this verified local executable contract; clean online dependency resolution, global installation and other-platform distribution are not claimed. (E-AH-INSTALL-01, E-AH-CLI-01, E-AH-STARTUP-01, E-AH-CONTRACT-01, E-AH-TEST-01, E-UP-CLI-01) |

### 3.2 Pi Web 0.9.3 to 0.10.0 delta

#### Pi 1.0 foundation

| ID | Status | Criterion and current evidence |
|---|---|---|
| WEB-BASE-01 | `PARTIAL` | Deliver the runtime capabilities introduced by the reference Web migration across session APIs, settings, tools, and resources through AiHarness Core/server contracts. AiHarness remains independent and now has generation-bounded provider/session hosting, native MCP configuration/Test contracts and a shared persistent direct-tool host used by detached Web runs, CLI and RPC. Shared deferred search and MCP OAuth now have behavioral coverage; the bounded executable QuickJS/nested foundation is now delivered, with model bridges and specialized progress/cost now delivered in E-AH-MOD-01 and E-AH-TOOL-03. Several migrated interaction capabilities remain incomplete; this does not close the broader foundation criterion. Core's shared deferred `tool_search` is delivered (E-AH-TOOL-01); QuickJS and scoped tool/code-mode controls are delivered in E-AH-TOOL-02; full extension-level built-in switches and extension-owned MCP registration remain open. Each application generation owns its services, credentials and provider-network scope; replacement retires old handlers, in-flight writes and upgrades and closes sockets/transports with deadlines. Provider/SSE/WebSocket inactivity and output are bounded, with idempotent abort/cleanup tests. (E-UP-WEB-03, E-AH-MCP-09, E-AH-WEB-04, E-AH-RUNTIME-01, E-AH-TEST-01) |

#### MCP management and runtime

Browser cases exhaust every typed file-problem reason and reject source messages, while the reviewed full-state Light/Dark desktop/mobile baselines cover responsive presentation. (E-UP-WEB-02, E-UP-WEB-04, E-AH-MCP-01, E-AH-MCP-02, E-AH-MCP-05, E-AH-TEST-01) |

Server rejects browser-supplied configuration objects and returns a fresh redacted inventory plus selected scope/name; the bilingual focus-trapped Settings flow covers completion, secret/remote/fresh-project confirmation, original-text submission, canonical success and failure reload across responsive baselines and browser tests. A confirmed fresh first write serializes exact trust and import with rollback; no resolver, command execution, network, OAuth or credential-store access is reachable. (E-UP-WEB-04, E-AH-MCP-03, E-AH-MCP-04, E-AH-MCP-07, E-AH-TEST-01) |
| WEB-MCP-05 | `DONE` | Detect literal credentials, escape literals, preserve supported references, collect missing values, optionally reference confirmed host variables, reject reserved server-authentication variables, and restrict literal-secret entries to global scope. Server passes environment names only; API, Core and browser negatives prove values/configuration never return and unconfirmed requests do not write. The transaction holds the heartbeat trust-store lock, persists one exact allow, revalidates identity/resources/decisions/breadth after acquiring the MCP lock, and restores the exact prior decision map on write failure without overwriting a cooperating mutation; rollback failure is typed as `trustKept`. Server accepts literal `trustProject: true` only for a project import at `revision: null`, while Settings requires a distinct accessible confirmation, never updates trust optimistically, closes stale controls and reloads authoritative inventory plus workspace trust after success or failure. Cross-manager serialization, nested decisions, bounds, links, races, rollback, strict payload, browser and live-server tests close the contract. Full replacement reparses the original bounded text, reapplies field/reference/secret/confirmation policy without reading old hidden values, targets one existing valid scope/name/revision, supports collision-safe in-place rename, preserves roots/siblings/order/style/mode, and leaves structural no-ops byte-stable. Server rejects unknown or smuggled configuration, returns only canonical redacted inventory plus minimal identity, and Web disables competing edits, reloads after every failure or uncertain result and restores canonical row focus. Links, malformed/stale/invalid/missing/colliding targets and ordinary untrusted project writes fail closed; the only exception is the separately confirmed, bounded and rollback-safe fresh first import. Core/Server/client/browser tests and four reviewed responsive baselines cover the completed local edit contract. (E-UP-WEB-04, E-AH-MCP-02, E-AH-MCP-03, E-AH-MCP-04, E-AH-MCP-05, E-AH-MCP-06, E-AH-MCP-07, E-AH-TEST-01) |

Settings serializes every local action, submits only exact enum exposure values, disables competing controls, restores focus after the canonical response, and reloads after deterministic or uncertain failure; row/group activation, focus-trapped removal, 60-second one-use server-side undo and ordered partial outcomes retain their prior coverage. Core treats omitted exposure as `direct`, deletes redundant `direct`, preserves secret-bearing siblings without returning them, no-ops unchanged requests, and reuses fresh trust, revision, lock, validation, mode/style and atomic-write policy. Server strictly rejects unknown payload fields, requires `configuration`, and returns only fresh redacted inventory; Core, Server, client, browser and four reviewed responsive baselines cover global/project, stale/invalid, error and focus paths. Identical tests join, command-backed tests serialize, every queue/request/run/close/page/item/output/status bound is finite, and an unshared subscriber disconnect aborts the flight. The tester lists but never invokes tools, never starts OAuth or resolves `oauth.clientSecret`, redacts literal/interpolated/command secrets from all server-controlled output, reports challenges as `needs-auth`, closes independently, and stores only bounded in-memory status keyed to the current configuration. The strict `configuration` route accepts only `{scope,name,cwd?}`; Settings distinguishes Configured from Connected, can test a disabled valid entry, and renders identity, cwd, capabilities, tools/read-only/exposure, redacted stderr/errors and timeout/auth state with browser and reviewed responsive evidence. It owns one exact record per session target, reacts to tool-list and close/error notifications, retries on a later prompt, begins idle cleanup only after `finishPrompt`, and participates in bounded CLI/RPC/Server shutdown. | WEB-CHAT-02 | `DONE` | Stage an edit-from-history branch and move the branch only when the edited message is sent, so cancel/reload leaves history unchanged. Edit only loads text and a transient target into the current composer; cancel restores the previous draft/focus, and navigation/reload clears the target without server mutations. Send forks immediately before the durable user entry, excludes its old version, then submits literal edited text on the new session without changing the source leaf. Core/HTTP and four desktop/mobile Light/Dark journeys cover empty prefixes, retained commands, reload/cancel, exact requests, focus, axe and overflow, with four reviewed baselines. (E-UP-WEB-03, E-AH-SESSION-01) |
| WEB-CHAT-03 | `DONE` | Let reasoning level change during a run and apply it from the next model request. Core resolves thinking before each provider turn, including post-tool and queued-message turns; Server rereads session/project/global/environment precedence, null inheritance and max-to-xhigh mapping without changing an in-flight call. Core/HTTP negatives and the streaming browser journey cover patch/reset and the next request; responsive edit journeys expose the enabled reasoning selector. Bilingual guidance explains the boundary. (E-UP-WEB-03, E-AH-THINK-01) |

One pure key classifier keeps `Shift+Enter` as a newline, submits Ctrl/Cmd+Enter only in modifier mode, and refuses active composition or Safari `keyCode === 229`; `ChatView` additionally suppresses submission during the 100 ms post-`compositionend` confirmation window. Unit, migration and browser tests cover both modes/newlines/IME, and six reviewed Light/Dark desktop/tablet/mobile preference baselines expose the setting. (E-UP-WEB-03, E-AH-WEB-01, E-AH-WEB-05, E-AH-TEST-01) |

Explicit scope/provenance and project-masking feedback, canonical readback and failure states are covered. ### 3.3 API and Contract Families

#### CLI contracts

| Target family | Reference capability | AiHarness evidence | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Process invocation | Strict options, bounded inputs, text/JSON/RPC modes, and top-level operations | Strict parser/startup adapters provide help/version, bounded prompt inputs, print, JSON, RPC, and interactive modes; regular/fullscreen runs also accept bounded steering and follow-ups while active. Strict process-only offline startup/catalogue/reload policy has public Core,actual headless/PTY and installed-bin evidence (E-AH-OFFLINE-01);discovery-only Markdown suppression now has public Core/SDK/installed/all-mode/PTY proof (E-AH-MARKDOWN-DISCOVERY-01). Definitive credential authority and the complete eight-clause startup-option reconciliation close CLI-RUN-07 (E-AH-CREDENTIAL-AUTHORITY-01);several operations,RPC/session and renderer capabilities remain open | `PARTIAL` |
| Interactive commands | Built-in operation catalogue plus resource/extension commands, completion, and `!`/`!!` shell entry | `commands/handler.ts`, `resources/resource-manager.ts`, the Core-backed CLI shell controller, and Core's shared queue scheduler cover a substantial AiHarness catalogue and active-run messaging; several operations and TUI behaviors remain open | `PARTIAL` |
| JSON events | Documented agent/message/tool/session JSONL event union | `cli/json-output.ts` emits a versioned header and broad agent/turn/message/tool/retry/compaction lifecycle with protocol-pure serialized output; the full union and cross-mode behavior remain incomplete | `PARTIAL` |
| RPC | Correlated commands, responses, asynchronous events, lifecycle controls, and strict framing | `rpc/{rpc-server,rpc-controller,rpc-extension-ui}.ts` implements broad state, prompt/queue, model, compaction, session/tree/export, bash, and UI commands while retaining legacy method/params input; complete validation, ordering, and high-level SDK integration remain open | `PARTIAL` |
| Session file | Versioned append-only header and parent-linked entry tree | AiHarness has a durable v2 parent journal with active/abandoned history, commands, compaction and branch summaries; stable atomic rewrites, inter-process locks and current/legacy/malformed/future migration fixtures are complete, while append-only metadata,labels/filter modes and remaining nonbranch lifecycle semantics stay open;explicit navigation/context,branch-safe private state and complete branch/clone cancellation/current-position semantics are separately delivered | `PARTIAL` |
| SDK/packages | Reusable factories, session/runtime events, model/auth/resource types, and in-memory operation | Core exports independently tested model/auth/runtime primitives plus the credential-private `SpecializedModels` SDK with built-entry/type probes, and now a cohesive API-1 session/resource factory facade with genuine memory/custom/durable lifecycle and declaration consumers; explicit capabilities prevent implied OAuth/discovery/executable loading | `DONE` |
| Extensions | Versioned API for events, UI, options, tools, providers, and MCP | AiHarness `ExtensionRegistry` and module loader cover part of this independent API; prompt-local MCP tools intentionally remain outside the mutable registry but use its hosted before-tool/event pipeline. Complete documented extension loading/discovery (CLI-EXT-01), owned startup options/help/completion, genuine archive/PATH-bin behavior and fresh ESM/CommonJS entries are delivered. Complete branch-safe session/custom state,persistence and mode-specific generations now include all startup ID/prefix/file copies,read-only native snapshots and captured destination authority under DEC-90,after preserving DEC-89's retraction (E-AH-STARTUP-FORK-STATE-01);bounded ordered branch/explicit-summary hooks and independent host borrowing are delivered separately by DEC-91 (E-AH-SESSION-BRANCH-01);extension-owned MCP registration and remaining lifecycle/UI coverage stay incomplete | `PARTIAL` |

#### Web delta routes and contracts

| Pi Web 0.10.0 family | Reference capability or route | Closest AiHarness surface | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| MCP overview/import/mutations | `GET/POST /api/mcp` | `GET /api/mcp`, admin `POST /api/mcp/preview`, and admin `POST /api/mcp/actions` provide redacted inventory, non-mutating paste conversion, confirmation-gated single-entry import/full-entry replacement and strict activation/exposure/removal/restore actions | `DONE` |
| MCP test | `POST /api/mcp/test` | Protected no-store `POST /api/mcp/test` accepts only `{scope,name,cwd?}`, rereads the file in Core and returns one bounded redacted connection/capability result with volatile config-keyed status | `DONE` |
| MCP agent host | Pre-prompt per-session hosting and direct tool invocation | The Server composition root injects Core's shared `McpSessionHost` into `AgentRuntime`; detached runs reread exact config/trust before prompts, await only direct startup, preserve/replace records safely, enforce selection/read-only policy, invoke bounded redacted tools and publish volatile config-keyed state. Administration routes never trigger hosting | `DONE` |
| MCP OAuth | `POST /api/mcp/sign-in`, flow GET/POST/DELETE, sign-out mutation | Administrator `POST /api/mcp/oauth/status|start|logout`, flow GET/DELETE and POST pasted redirect adapt Core's encrypted detached flow/passive refresh contracts. Behavioral evidence, bilingual user/operator docs and eight versioned responsive OAuth baselines are delivered | `DONE` |
| Project trust | Trust mutations with MCP inventory and fresh exact/inherited/refused decisions | `GET/POST/DELETE /api/workspaces/trust` shares Core's exact/inherited/refused resource status; workspace descriptors distinguish resource-sensitive `trusted` from persisted-decision `operationTrusted`, and a confirmed project import can serialize one exact fresh-folder allow with its first write and rollback | `DONE` |
| Code mode/tool settings | `GET/PUT /api/tools/settings` for defaultTools and codemode settings | AiHarness `GET/PATCH /api/tools` supplies cached health, scoped activation/presentation/budget, selected tools, visible reasons and canonical save/readback with atomic validation | `DONE` |
| Default model/reasoning | `PUT /api/models/default` | Strict independent `PATCH /api/configuration/defaults` model/thinking intents and fresh scoped storage/readback preserve the other key and current sessions, with inheritance/provenance/masking and unreadable-config refusal | `DONE` |
| Enabled resources | Batch plugin/skill routes and grouped model operations | Bounded `/api/plugins/enabled` and exact provider/displayed `/api/models/enabled/batch` are delivered; grouped skill frontmatter editing remains separate from `/api/skills/enabled` | `PARTIAL` |
| Directory picker | `/api/cwd/browse`, `/api/default-cwd`, direct-child creation | `GET /api/workspaces/{roots,default,browse}`, `POST /api/workspaces/{validate,browse}`; Core caps listings at 500 with `truncated`, and strict POST creates one canonical direct child. Defaults/custom paths share canonical directory validation, display-only home labels and revalidated persistence; unavailable defaults have explicit recovery without implicit root/trust grants | `DONE` |
| Host explorer | `POST /api/open-in-explorer` | `GET|POST /api/workspaces/file-manager` exposes loopback/platform availability and opens only a canonical allowed directory through Core's bounded shell-free launcher | `DONE` |
| Worktree removal | Explicit dirty-worktree force retry | `DELETE /api/workspaces/worktrees` returns `force-required`, accepts only Boolean `force`, and removes only after a second explicit Web confirmation | `DONE` |
| Linked directories/files | `/api/files/*` explicit linked-target allowance and Git-aware visibility | Existing Git-aware visibility remains; `/api/workspaces/linked-directories`, `/consent`, `/browse` and `/content` supply independent Linux-scoped confirmed reads with fresh trust/identity and no execution/root grant. Dynamic session-evidence file authority remains open under WEB-FILE-06 | `PARTIAL` |
| Live session changes | Long-lived session runtime, event stream, active-run fork, and mutable thinking | AiHarness `/api/agent/*` and `/api/sessions/*` now have bounded replay, generation-safe replacement and shutdown; stable persisted prefixes can be copied while active, edits fork only on Send and reasoning resolves at each next provider call; complete prompt/phase/ordered-extension semantics remain open | `PARTIAL` |
| Static demo | `demo/` application and Pages workflow | Separate synthetic/offline React/Vite demo and guarded base-safe Pages pipeline; static artifact/browser proof exists but no remote deployment is claimed | `PARTIAL` |

## 4. Convention for Future Audits

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `ABSENT` | Required functional deliverable or evidence does not exist. |
| `PARTIAL` | A demonstrable foundation exists, but the full criterion is unmet. |
| `TO BE DEFINED` | A product, security, or scope decision is required. |
| `IN PROGRESS` | Implementation or verification is actively underway and linked in the journal. |
| `BLOCKED` | An external blocker, owner, and release trigger are documented. |
| `DONE` | The complete criterion is verified at the pinned target with evidence and applicable tests/visuals. |
| `GAP ACCEPTED` | A dated final decision intentionally declines the criterion with justification and a re-examination trigger. |

### Closure Rule

Move an item to `DONE` only after all behavior named in its row is implemented through documented AiHarness contracts, relevant state migrations are tested, security-negative paths pass, documentation is updated, applicable desktop/mobile visuals are reviewed, and the journal records commands actually run. `PARTIAL` is never closed. Mark this report `DONE` only when every criterion is `DONE` or `GAP ACCEPTED`.

## 5. Decisions and Accepted Gaps

### Audit decisions

| Decision | Date | Rule applied | Consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| DEC-01 | 2026-10-03 | Pin tag objects and peeled commits, never floating tag names. | All findings remain reproducible even if a tag or default branch moves. |
| DEC-02 | 2026-10-03 | Keep Pi CLI parity and the Pi Web `0.9.3..0.10.0` delta as separate inventories. | The prior Web baseline is not mixed into CLI counts or silently re-audited. |

| DEC-04 | 2026-10-03 | Treat `docs/audit/pi/AUDIT-1` as a declared closed baseline, not detailed inherited evidence. | Existing foundations can justify `PARTIAL`; they do not automatically make new delta criteria `DONE`. |

| DEC-06 | 2026-10-03 | Do not require upstream component filenames. | Missing `WorkspacePicker.tsx` and `SkillSettings.tsx` are negative inventory facts, not standalone gaps when behavior exists elsewhere. |

## 7. Glossary and Prefixes

| Prefix/term | Scope/definition | Example |
|---|---|---|
| `CLI-RUN-*` | CLI process entry, modes, flags, and built-in command surface | CLI-RUN-04 |
| `CLI-TUI-*` | Interactive terminal editor, transcript, display, and input behavior | CLI-TUI-07 |
| `CLI-MOD-*` | Providers, models, credentials, thinking, and usage | CLI-MOD-03 |
| `CLI-CFG-*` | User/project configuration, context, system prompts, and settings | CLI-CFG-02 |
| `CLI-SES-*` | Session persistence, tree, compaction, import/export, and recovery | CLI-SES-04 |
| `CLI-TOOL-*` | Built-in tools, shell, codemode, tool search, and MCP | CLI-TOOL-08 |
| `CLI-RES-*` | Skills, prompt templates, and themes | CLI-RES-01 |
| `CLI-PKG-*` | AiHarness package lifecycle | CLI-PKG-01 |
| `CLI-EXT-*` | Extension loading and runtime contracts | CLI-EXT-02 |
| `CLI-AUT-*` | JSON mode, RPC mode, SDK, and public integration contracts | CLI-AUT-02 |
| `CLI-SEC-*` | Trust, paths, secrets, execution, and process boundaries | CLI-SEC-01 |
| `WEB-BASE-*` | Pi 1.0 migration foundations introduced by Pi Web 0.10.0 | WEB-BASE-01 |
| `WEB-MCP-*` | MCP configuration, security, management, OAuth, and runtime | WEB-MCP-11 |
| `WEB-TOOL-*` | Codemode, tool search, exposure, progress, and extension UI queue | WEB-TOOL-02 |
| `WEB-FILE-*` | Directory selection, file access, links, explorer, and worktrees | WEB-FILE-05 |
| `WEB-CHAT-*` | Chat composer, session branching, events, mobile, and rendering | WEB-CHAT-04 |
| `WEB-SET-*` | Settings UI, trust, models, plugins, skills, and localization | WEB-SET-06 |
| `WEB-OPS-*` | Demo, release workflow, lifecycle hardening, tests, and docs | WEB-OPS-01 |
| Functional parity | Equivalent observable capability and safety through AiHarness-owned interfaces; internal and public representations may differ | Session branching behavior |
| Contract quality | Documented AiHarness inputs, outputs, sequencing, persisted schema, and extension/SDK behavior | AiHarness RPC |
| Direct tool | A tool schema declared directly to the model; the runtime waits for a connecting MCP server that must expose one | WEB-MCP-12 |
| Deferred exposure | Metadata first; authenticated discovery may declare the eligible tool on the next provider turn | WEB-TOOL-03 |
| Codemode exposure | Script-only even after discovery; separate from activation or authorization of the QuickJS helper | CLI-TOOL-06 |
| Hidden exposure | Neither declared, discoverable nor nested-callable; exact remote-name overrides may replace a server default | WEB-TOOL-03 |
